                    Supine Cross Connect                                           2          5                                     --:-- min
- 2 round           Rolling Arm Bar w/ Screwdriver Rotation                        2          5                                     --:-- min
                    Walking Sprinters Mobility                                     2          8                                     --:-- min
                    Extensive Locomotion Cariocas                                  2                                     00:02:30   1:00 min
- 2 round           Backwards Extensive Locomotive Skips                           2                                     00:02:30   1:00 min
                    Extensive Locomotive Skips                                     2                                     00:02:30   1:00 min
                    Back Leg Lunge (Peterson Lunge)                                2          5                                     1:00 min
- 2 round
                    Wide Stance Length Under Load                                  2                                     00:00:30   --:-- min

Staff Member Notes:
Optional Foam Roll Circuit:

25-45 seconds each

-Glutes, hamstrings, calves, quads/hip flexors, lacrosse or golf ball on bottom of feet.

Supine Cross Connect:

reps = breaths (inhale through the nose, long exhale out the mouth).

Rolling Arm Bar w/ Screwdriver Rotation :

This drill works miracles for building hip internal rotation mobility, but the details can be a bit tricky. It’s ok if you don’t get it down day 1.

Follow the directions in the video but I want you to especially think about the following tips when you perform the exercise.

The leg that is down on the ground must be actively pushed into the floor throughout the entire exercise. Don’t lose pressure in that leg or you won’t get the
hip mobility benefits.

Breath in through the nose as you roll over, and out through the mouth when you roll back to the starting position.

Keep the knee that is down fully locked out and the foot dorsiflexed (toes pulled up towards your head).

Make sure the additional weight is light, between 10 and 20 lbs.

Walking Sprinters Mobility:

1 set = completion of recommended reps on each leg.

*Exercise idea inspired by compound performance

Extensive Locomotion Cariocas:

As always, if this exercise cause pain, skip it and move on to the next exercise.

Level 1 = foot cycles over ANKLE level (low)

Level 2 = foot cycles over MID SHIN level (medium)

Level 3 = foot cycles over KNEE level (High)

Level 4 = weighted vest or medicine ball + KNEE level cycle (High)

Backwards Extensive Locomotive Skips :

Level 1 = backwards skips foot coming over ANKLE
Level 2 = backwards skips foot coming over MID SHIN
Level 3 = backwards skips foot coming over KNEE
Level 4 = WEIGHTED vest or dumbbells + backwards skips foot coming over KNEE
Staff Member Notes:
Extensive Locomotive Skips:

As always, if this exercise cause pain, skip it and move on to the next exercise.

Level 1 = foot cycles over ANKLE level (low)

Level 2 = foot cycles over MID SHIN level (medium)

Level 3 = foot cycles at KNEE level (High)

Level 4 = weighted vest or medicine ball + KNEE level cycle (High)

Wide Stance Length Under Load :

If this exercise causes discomfort or pain, or simply makes you feel even more tight after you finish you may not need additional hamstring stretching and you
can skip this exercise.



Week 6, Day 4 - Lower Body
Workout Date: ________________                                                                                      Total Workout Time: ________________

Workout Recommendations
Time to get some BIG GAINS! Let's get to work!! A few notes for today’s workout before you get started:
-I don't want you to get overly sore from this workout. We want to feel a LITTLE soreness tomorrow, but if we're overly sore then we did too much too soon.
-As always, skip any exercise (or choose the regression) if it causes pain or discomfort.
-If you're advanced and have been highly active for the last month, it's ok to add sets wherever you feel you need for some extra work.
-Remember, you have to train according to your current level. This is a general blueprint, but YOU have to know when to skip certain things that may not fit
your current level. Training hard is important, but training SMART is key!

The journey begins now! Let's GO!

                                               Exercise                              Sets    Reps Weight Distance         Time      Rest           Notes
Placeholder     Nasal Breathing Cardio Warm Up                                           1                              00:05:00    --:-- min
                Split Stance Loaded Hip Rotations                                        1       8                                  --:-- min
                Hip Shift Squat                                                          2       6                                  --:-- min
                 Alternate: Hip Shift Mechanics (Alternate)                              2      6,6                                 --:-- min
                Staggered Stance RDL w/ Same Side Load                                   2        6                                 --:-- min
- 2 round

Staff Member Notes:
Nasal Breathing Cardio Warm Up:

If you're pain free, you can use a fast walk/jog as your warmup. Start slow and gradually ramp it up to a jogging pace. However, if you haven't been running
consistently or you have pain when running, then you can use a fast walk OR a stationary bike, elliptical or any other low impact cardio machine.

Split Stance Loaded Hip Rotations :

-Use a light dumbbell or any form of additional weight 5-15 lbs is plenty of weight.

-Hold onto a med ball or bench with the off hand for some added stability.

-Complete the reps with right leg forward and right hand, THEN right leg forward left hand, THEN left leg forward left hand, THEN left leg forward right hand.
That completes 1 set.

-For example, if it says 8 reps that means RIGHT LEG forward with band around right thigh-- do 8 reps right hand THEN 8 reps left hand. Then switch the band
to the left leg, put your left leg forward and complete 8 reps left hand then 8 reps right hand. A total of 32 repetitions per set.

Hip Shift Squat:

If this position is too difficult for you to master, you can use this regression-- Click "alternate exercise" to view".

Half Kneeling Hip Flexor Stretch:

1 set = completing recommended reps to both sides.

If you don't have enough flexibility to grab your foot, you can use the alternate exercise as a regression for 5 reps (breaths). Click "alternate exercise" to view.
Staff Member Notes:
Ballistic Wall Side Hip Extension:

If you do not feel ready for the dynamic (fast) variation of this exercise. You can start slow and controlled.

Click to view the "alternative exercise" for be another beginner level alternative

Crossover Step Adductor Stretch:

1 rep = over and back

If this is too advanced or causes pain, you can try the static adductor stretch progressions instead. Click "alternate exercise" to view.

Curved Tempo Runs :

1 set = down 30 yards AND back 30 yards, THEN take your rest period before your next set.

Use 3 cones spaced 10 yards apart.

If you are 100% injury free and have been sprinting consistently, you can skip to 80-90% max speed in week 1. Then in week 2 ramp it up to 90-100%.

But if you have not been sprinting consistently and/or you're coming back from an injury you may want to start at 50% speed week 1, 60-70% week 2, 70-90%
week 3 and 90-100% week 4. Only progress intensities if you're pain free.

Short Accel/Decel Progressions:

Level 1= 5 yard acceleration + 5 yards of room to decelerate.

Level 2= 5 yard acceleration + only 2 yards of room to decelerate.

1 set = completion of recommended reps to one side , then completion of recommended reps to the other side.

Heels Elevated Narrow Squat:

Level 1 = heel elevated + assistance

Level 2 = heel elevated, bodyweight

Level 3 = heel elevated + dumbbell or kettlebell goblet position

Level 4 = heel elevated + barbell (front OR back squat position), whatever you're more comfortable with.

If level 1 causes pain, you can use less range of motion. If it still causes pain then regress back to isometrics for 2-3 sets of 45 second holds. Click "alternate
exercise" to view.
Staff Member Notes:
Altitude Drop Progressions:

-If you are able to jump maximally pain free, you can skip straight to expert level on this drill. However, if jumping and landing still causes pain in your joints
you may want to start at beginner/intermediate level and prove to yourself you can do this drill pain free.

-Beginner Level = Start at 50% of your standing vertical jump height, progress by 2 inches per week.

-Intermediate Level = Start at 50% of your standing vertical jump height, progress by 2 inches per week.

-Expert = Start with a box height of your standing vertical jump height. Progress box height by 4 inches per week.

Eccentric Nordic Hamstring Curl :

-Level 1 = perform the eccentric (lowering) but use your hands to push back up. Don’t perform the upward phase.

-Level 2 = perform the eccentric and the concentric but use a push off with the hands to help yourself get up.

-Level 3 = add weight on the way down, set the weight down and push off with your hands to get back up.

Hip Flexor Length Under Load:

Level 1 = knee stays on floor, hold onto something for assistance

Level 2 = knee comes off floor (holding in long split squat position), hold onto something for assistance. Find a depth that feels like a stretch but doesn't cause
pain or excessive discomfort

Level 3 = hold in long split squat, begin to oscillate (pulse) up and down just a few inches. We're still holding onto something for assistance.

Level 4 = same position as level 3, small up and down pulse but NO hand assistance.

Level 5 = lightly weighted. Grab a dumbbell or kettlebell and hold it in your hands between your legs (under your hips). Pulse up and down a few inches.

Level 6 = same as level 5, except we use bumper plates or small boxes to stand on in order to get deeper range of motion.

Level 7 = same as level 6, except we add a small turn towards the front leg to stretch the rotational fibers in the back hip flexor.

Regression if these cause pain/discomfort--Click "alternate exercise" to view.

Rotational RDL Length Under Load :

If this exercise causes discomfort or pain, you can skip it or try an unloaded post chain stretch as a regression. Click "alternate exercise" to view.
Staff Member Notes:
Optional Pigeon Length Under Load :

-Work through these progressions slowly. Level 3 and 4 can be very difficult for many athletes.

Level 1 = lying on your back, figure 4 stretch.

Level 2 = pigeon stretch with extended back leg, heavy on hands, slowly lower chest towards knee.

Level 3 = same position, remove hands from floor and keep hands straight out to your side. Lower yourself slowly until chest reaches your knee, slowly raise
back up.

Level 4 = hands to side, lift out and bring back leg around to reach tall kneeling position. Slowly reverse the movement and lower back to starting position.

-Only move onto the next level when you’ve mastered the form of the previous variation and you feel like you need a new challenge

Wide Stance Length Under Load :

If this exercise causes discomfort or pain, or simply makes you feel even more tight after you finish you may not need additional hamstring stretching and you
can skip this exercise.

Level 1 = elbows resting on couch, bench or chair.

Level 2 = elbows off, now upper back is resting on couch, bench or chair.

Level 3 = remove couch, bench or chair. Extend arms and use hands to support you on the floor.

Level 4 = use elbows/forearms to support you on the floor.

Some people experience knee pain with these variations. If level 1 knee pain or any other joint pain, use this regression instead-- Click "alternate exercise" to
view.

Staff Member Notes:
Nasal Breathing Cardio Warm Up:

If you're pain free, you can use a fast walk/jog as your warmup. Start slow and gradually ramp it up to a jogging pace. However, if you haven't been running
consistently or you have pain when running, then you can use a fast walk OR a stationary bike, elliptical or any other low impact cardio machine.

Split Stance Loaded Hip Rotations :

-Use a light dumbbell or any form of additional weight 5-15 lbs is plenty of weight.

-Hold onto a med ball or bench with the off hand for some added stability.

-Complete the reps with right leg forward and right hand, THEN right leg forward left hand, THEN left leg forward left hand, THEN left leg forward right hand.
That completes 1 set.

-For example, if it says 8 reps that means RIGHT LEG forward with band around right thigh-- do 8 reps right hand THEN 8 reps left hand. Then switch the band
to the left leg, put your left leg forward and complete 8 reps left hand then 8 reps right hand. A total of 32 repetitions per set.

Hip Shift Squat:

If this position is too difficult for you to master, you can use this regression-- Click "alternate exercise" to view".
Staff Member Notes:
Half Kneeling Hip Flexor Stretch:

1 set = completing recommended reps to both sides.

If you don't have enough flexibility to grab your foot, you can use the alternate exercise as a regression for 5 reps (breaths). Click "alternate exercise" to view.

Ballistic Wall Side Hip Extension:

If you do not feel ready for the dynamic (fast) variation of this exercise. You can start slow and controlled.

Click to view the "alternative exercise" for be another beginner level alternative

Hip Loaded Diagonal Jumps :

Level 1 = bodyweight. Start with small side to side jumps and gradually increase distance.

Level 2 = lightly loaded. 5-10 lb dumbbells.

1 repetition = over and back (left and right side)

Mutli-direction Bounds:

If this is too difficult for you, regress back to this Euro Step Balance drill. Click "alternate exercise" to view.

RDL Row w/ Rotation :

If this variation is too difficult or you don't have access to a cable machine you can use the 1 leg dumbbell RDL as a regression. Click "alternate exercise" to
view.

Knee Step Overs:

Level 1 = bodyweight with small range of motion and small step length. Gradually work to get more range of motion and longer step length.

Level 2 = dumbbells in each hand 5-25 lbs to start.

Level 3 = keep the same dumbbell weight and add a resistance band pulling you forward (or use a cable machine/belt around hips).

1 set = completion of recommended reps on each side
Staff Member Notes:
Lateral Crossover Sled Drag Fatigue/Coordination :

Perform the sled crossover drag (or band crossover as a substitution) for the recommended reps (measured in time) on BOTH sides. Then without taking a
break, immediately begin the carioca to challenge coordination under fatigue.

Remember, the carioca coordination drill is performed for HALF the reps (time) of the weighted drag. So if the recommended reps said 40 seconds, that means
40 seconds down with the weighted drag, switch sides, 40 seconds back. Then 20 seconds on the unloaded carioca. Then take your rest period before
beginning your next set.

If you don't have the sled, perform with a band (have a friend walk with you resisting you with the band). Or you can do a stationary cable or band crossover
step, come back to starting position then repeat.

Hip Flexor Length Under Load:

Level 1 = knee stays on floor, hold onto something for assistance

Level 2 = knee comes off floor (holding in long split squat position), hold onto something for assistance. Find a depth that feels like a stretch but doesn't cause
pain or excessive discomfort

Level 3 = hold in long split squat, begin to oscillate (pulse) up and down just a few inches. We're still holding onto something for assistance.

Level 4 = same position as level 3, small up and down pulse but NO hand assistance.

Level 5 = lightly weighted. Grab a dumbbell or kettlebell and hold it in your hands between your legs (under your hips). Pulse up and down a few inches.

Level 6 = same as level 5, except we use bumper plates or small boxes to stand on in order to get deeper range of motion.

Level 7 = same as level 6, except we add a small turn towards the front leg to stretch the rotational fibers in the back hip flexor.

Regression if these cause pain/discomfort--Click "alternate exercise" to view.

Wide Stance Length Under Load :

If this exercise causes discomfort or pain, or simply makes you feel even more tight after you finish you may not need additional hamstring stretching and you
can skip this exercise.

Rotational RDL Length Under Load :

If this exercise causes discomfort or pain, you can skip it or try an unloaded post chain stretch as a regression. Click "alternate exercise" to view.
Staff Member Notes:
Lateral Lunge Length Under Load :

Level 1 = feet on floor

Level 2 = non loaded leg elevated on bumper plate or small step 3-6 inches

Level 3 = non loaded leg elevated on box, bench or chair around mid shin to knee height

Level 4 = non loaded leg elevated on box, bench or chair around mid shin to knee height AND the loaded leg elevated on bumper plate or small step 3-6
inches.

If these variations are too difficult or cause pain/excess discomfort, use this regression instead. Click "alternate exercise" to view.

Optional Pigeon Length Under Load :

-Work through these progressions slowly. Level 3 and 4 can be very difficult for many athletes.

Level 1 = lying on your back, figure 4 stretch.

Level 2 = pigeon stretch with extended back leg, heavy on hands, slowly lower chest towards knee.

Level 3 = same position, remove hands from floor and keep hands straight out to your side. Lower yourself slowly until chest reaches your knee, slowly raise
back up.

Level 4 = hands to side, lift out and bring back leg around to reach tall kneeling position. Slowly reverse the movement and lower back to starting position.

-Only move onto the next level when you’ve mastered the form of the previous variation and you feel like you need a new challenge

Long Length Hamstring Rapid Loading :

Curved Tempo Runs :

1 set = down 30 yards AND back 30 yards, THEN take your rest period before your next set.

Use 3 cones spaced 10 yards apart.

If you are 100% injury free and have been sprinting consistently, you can skip to 80-90% max speed in week 1. Then in week 2 ramp it up to 90-100%.

But if you have not been sprinting consistently and/or you're coming back from an injury you may want to start at 50% speed week 1, 60-70% week 2, 70-90%
week 3 and 90-100% week 4. Only progress intensities if you're pain free.

Week 7, Day 2 - Core/ Upper Body
Workout Date: ________________                                                                                     Total Workout Time: ________________

Workout Recommendations
-Today's first workout is focused on upper body and core and the second workout is focused on light full body mobility to drive blood flow and synovial fluid.
-Ideally these two workouts will be separated by at least 6 hours. However, if you don't have the time to separate them, you can perform both workouts at the
same time. Use workout 2 as a warmup for workout 1 if you cannot separate them. The reason we separate them is because it's good for your body to
accumulate movement throughout the day instead of all at once.
-For the isometrics in workout #2, I want you to get through at least 2 sets, but if you have no pain and you're not overly sore from previous lower body days
then you can do up to 4 sets.
Let's get to work!

Staff Member Notes:
90/90 Knee Roll:

Don't worry about counting breaths here. You may simply set your timer and get those hips moving!

*Exercise inspired by Postural Restoration Institute and David Grey
Staff Member Notes:
All Four Prone Hip Shift Drill :

1 rep = over and back.

If this drill is too difficult you can use a supine 90/90 knee roll as a regression, just click to view the "alternative exercise".

Cable Rotation/Rib Dissociation:

-If you don’t have access to a cable, use a band for this exercise.

-You should feel a burn in the obliques (side abs), but we shouldn’t be working with too heavy of a weight.

-This exercise should feel rhythmic and fluid.

-Make sure your pelvis doesn’t rotate along with your ribs/spine. Pelvis has to stay locked in place in order to get the dissociation in the ribs/spine.

Bear Crawl (Movement):

4 steps forward (2 with each leg), the 4 steps backwards completes 1 repetition.

If this variation is too difficult or causes pain, you can click the "alternative exercise" to try an All Four Alternating Belly Lift.

*Rotational concepts discussed in the video are inspired by Weck Method.

Eyes Closed Diagonal Steps :

Level 1 = eyes closed SMALL steps

Level 2 = eyes closed MEDIUM sized steps

Level 3 = eyes closed BIG steps (small bound)

If you are high risk for falls, have poor balance or this exercise causes pain, skip this exercise and try a stationary regression. Click to view "alternate exercise".

Don't worry about counting reps, simply set your timer and get going!

Towel Curl:

If you don't have the towel you can use a rope extension for the cable machine. Or you can use a dumbbell incline curl if you do not have access to a cable
machine. Click "alternate exercise" to view.

Towel Tricep Extension:

If you don't have the towel, you can use a regular rope cable extension as seen in this video. Click "alternate exercise" to view.

Reps are counting in breaths

Staff Member Notes:
Toe Motor Control:

Curl the toes, hold for 2 seconds, then lift the toes as high as you can towards your head, hold for 2 seconds, then spread the toes as wide as possible, hold 2
seconds, then repeat the cycle. Perform as many reps as the total set time will allow for.

Wall Side Posture Reach:

*Exercise inspired by ELDOA Technique


Cat Camel:

1 rep = down (cat) and up (camel).

Griffith Wall Rotations:

1 set = completing recommended reps on both sides

*Exercise inspired by Vernon Griffith
Staff Member Notes:
Slant Board Isometric Progressions:

Elevate your heel on a weighted plate, small wedge or book if you don’t have a slant board

Level 1 = hand assistance for additional support

Level 2 = bodyweight only

Level 3 = hold a backpack or light dumbbells for additional weight

Always start with a VERY small knee bend. Once you prove you can complete the set pain free you may begin to bend the knee up to 60-80 degrees.

Only progress to the next level if you’re pain free and you are no longer getting an 8/10 burn in the quads by the end of the set

If the slant board iso is too difficult or causes pain, click "alternative exercise" to use the 60 degree iso hold as a regression. The same reps and sets apply to
the regression.

2 Leg Hamstring Iso:

-Lay on your back and bend your knees slightly.

-Drive your heels into the ground to lift your hips off the floor until you achieve full hip extension. Hold in this position for the recommended set time.

-You should feel the burn in your hamstrings, not your lower back. If you feel it in your lower back you are likely hyperextending the spine instead of extending
the hips.

-If you cannot achieve an 8/10 burn in the hamstrings with just your bodyweight, you can move to level 2 by adding weight on top of the hips.

-Find a weight that allows you to achieve an 8/10 burn with no pain in the knees and back

Resisted Ankle Circles:

-Use a PJF fabric mini band and tie it around a light weight. The weight must be very light to start— 2.5 to 5 lbs is ideal.

-Perform the recommended reps in a clockwise direction, then perform the reps in a counter clockwise direction. Then switch legs to complete the set.

-Only go up in weight if you can no longer achieve an 8/10 burn in the shin/ankle muscles. As always, scale back or skip the exercise if you experience pain in
the ankles.
Staff Member Notes:
Hip Flexor/Quad Bridge:

Level 1 = elbows resting on couch, bench or chair.

Level 2 = elbows off, now upper back is resting on couch, bench or chair.

Level 3 = remove couch, bench or chair. Extend arms and use hands to support you on the floor.

Level 4 = use elbows/forearms to support you on the floor.

Some people experience knee pain with these variations. If level 1 knee pain or any other joint pain, use this regression instead-- Click "alternate exercise" to
view.

reps = breaths (inhale through the nose, long exhale out the mouth).
Staff Member Notes:
Rolling Arm Bar w/ Screwdriver Rotation :

This drill works miracles for building hip internal rotation mobility, but the details can be a bit tricky. It’s ok if you don’t get it down day 1.

Follow the directions in the video but I want you to especially think about the following tips when you perform the exercise.

The leg that is down on the ground must be actively pushed into the floor throughout the entire exercise. Don’t lose pressure in that leg or you won’t get the
hip mobility benefits.

Breath in through the nose as you roll over, and out through the mouth when you roll back to the starting position.

Keep the knee that is down fully locked out and the foot dorsiflexed (toes pulled up towards your head).

Make sure the additional weight is light, between 10 and 20 lbs.

Walking Sprinters Mobility:

1 set = completion of recommended reps on each leg.

*Exercise idea inspired by compound performance

Extensive Locomotion Cariocas:

As always, if this exercise cause pain, skip it and move on to the next exercise.

Level 1 = foot cycles over ANKLE level (low)

Level 2 = foot cycles over MID SHIN level (medium)

Level 3 = foot cycles over KNEE level (High)

Level 4 = weighted vest or medicine ball + KNEE level cycle (High)

Backwards Extensive Locomotive Skips :

Level 1 = backwards skips foot coming over ANKLE
Level 2 = backwards skips foot coming over MID SHIN
Level 3 = backwards skips foot coming over KNEE
Level 4 = WEIGHTED vest or dumbbells + backwards skips foot coming over KNEE
Staff Member Notes:
Extensive Locomotive Skips:

As always, if this exercise cause pain, skip it and move on to the next exercise.

Level 1 = foot cycles over ANKLE level (low)

Level 2 = foot cycles over MID SHIN level (medium)

Level 3 = foot cycles at KNEE level (High)

Level 4 = weighted vest or medicine ball + KNEE level cycle (High)

Wide Stance Length Under Load :

If this exercise causes discomfort or pain, or simply makes you feel even more tight after you finish you may not need additional hamstring stretching and you
can skip this exercise.



Week 7, Day 4 - Lower Body
Workout Date: ________________                                                                                      Total Workout Time: ________________

Workout Recommendations

Time to get some BIG GAINS! Let's get to work!! A few notes for today’s workout before you get started:
-I don't want you to get overly sore from this workout. We want to feel a LITTLE soreness tomorrow, but if we're overly sore then we did too much too soon.
-As always, skip any exercise (or choose the regression) if it causes pain or discomfort.
-If you're advanced and have been highly active for the last month, it's ok to add sets wherever you feel you need for some extra work.
-Remember, you have to train according to your current level. This is a general blueprint, but YOU have to know when to skip certain things that may not fit
your current level. Training hard is important, but training SMART is key!

The journey begins now! Let's GO!

Placeholder     Nasal Breathing Cardio Warm Up                                           1                                          --:-- min
                Split Stance Loaded Hip Rotations                                        1       8                                  --:-- min
                Hip Shift Squat                                                          2       6                                  --:-- min

- 2 round
                Half Kneeling Hip Flexor Stretch                                         1       8                                  --:-- min
                 Alternate: Hip Flexor w/ Rotation/Breathing (Alternate)                 1       8                                  --:-- min Perform each side
                Ballistic Wall Side Hip Extension                                        2      10                                  --:-- min
                 Alternate: Cross Connect Glute Bridge (Alternate)                       2   10,10                                  --:-- min
                Single Leg Hip IR/ER                                                     2       8                                  --:-- min
                Walking Sprinters Mobility                                               2       5                                  --:-- min
- 2 round
                 Crossover Step Adductor Stretch                                               2                                00:00:30   --:-- min
                  Alternate: Optional Adductor Stretch Progressions (Alternate)                2                                           --:-- min
                 Ankle Mobility 1 Leg Squat                                                    2      12                                   --:-- min

                 RFESS Progessions                                                             4       4                                   1:00 min
- 4 round         Alternate: 90 Degree Knee ISOs (Alternate)                                   4 4,4,4,4                                    --:-- min

Staff Member Notes:
Nasal Breathing Cardio Warm Up:

If you're pain free, you can use a fast walk/jog as your warmup. Start slow and gradually ramp it up to a jogging pace. However, if you haven't been running
consistently or you have pain when running, then you can use a fast walk OR a stationary bike, elliptical or any other low impact cardio machine.

Split Stance Loaded Hip Rotations :

-Use a light dumbbell or any form of additional weight 5-15 lbs is plenty of weight.

-Hold onto a med ball or bench with the off hand for some added stability.

-Complete the reps with right leg forward and right hand, THEN right leg forward left hand, THEN left leg forward left hand, THEN left leg forward right hand.
That completes 1 set.

-For example, if it says 8 reps that means RIGHT LEG forward with band around right thigh-- do 8 reps right hand THEN 8 reps left hand. Then switch the band
to the left leg, put your left leg forward and complete 8 reps left hand then 8 reps right hand. A total of 32 repetitions per set.

Hip Shift Squat:

If this position is too difficult for you to master, you can use this regression-- Click "alternate exercise" to view".
Staff Member Notes:
Half Kneeling Hip Flexor Stretch:

1 set = completing recommended reps to both sides.

If you don't have enough flexibility to grab your foot, you can use the alternate exercise as a regression for 5 reps (breaths). Click "alternate exercise" to view.

Ballistic Wall Side Hip Extension:

If you do not feel ready for the dynamic (fast) variation of this exercise. You can start slow and controlled.

Click to view the "alternative exercise" for be another beginner level alternative

Walking Sprinters Mobility:

1 set = completion of recommended reps on each leg.

*Exercise idea inspired by compound performance

Crossover Step Adductor Stretch:

1 rep = over and back

If this is too advanced or causes pain, you can try the static adductor stretch progressions instead. Click "alternate exercise" to view.

Curved Tempo Runs :

1 set = down 30 yards AND back 30 yards, THEN take your rest period before your next set.

Use 3 cones spaced 10 yards apart.

If you are 100% injury free and have been sprinting consistently, you can skip to 80-90% max speed in week 1. Then in week 2 ramp it up to 90-100%.

But if you have not been sprinting consistently and/or you're coming back from an injury you may want to start at 50% speed week 1, 60-70% week 2, 70-90%
week 3 and 90-100% week 4. Only progress intensities if you're pain free.


Altitude Drop Progressions:

-If you are able to jump maximally pain free, you can skip straight to expert level on this drill. However, if jumping and landing still causes pain in your joints
you may want to start at beginner/intermediate level and prove to yourself you can do this drill pain free.

-Beginner Level = Start at 50% of your standing vertical jump height, progress by 2 inches per week.

-Intermediate Level = Start at 50% of your standing vertical jump height, progress by 2 inches per week.

-Expert = Start with a box height of your standing vertical jump height. Progress box height by 4 inches per week.
Staff Member Notes:
RFESS Progessions:

If this exercise causes pain or discomfort, you can regress back to isometrics. Click "alternate exercise" to view.

Eccentric Nordic Hamstring Curl :

-Level 1 = perform the eccentric (lowering) but use your hands to push back up. Don’t perform the upward phase.

-Level 2 = perform the eccentric and the concentric but use a push off with the hands to help yourself get up.

-Level 3 = add weight on the way down, set the weight down and push off with your hands to get back up.

Wide Stance Length Under Load :

If this exercise causes discomfort or pain, or simply makes you feel even more tight after you finish you may not need additional hamstring stretching and you
can skip this exercise.

Lateral Lunge Length Under Load :

Level 1 = feet on floor

Level 2 = non loaded leg elevated on bumper plate or small step 3-6 inches

Level 3 = non loaded leg elevated on box, bench or chair around mid shin to knee height

Level 4 = non loaded leg elevated on box, bench or chair around mid shin to knee height AND the loaded leg elevated on bumper plate or small step 3-6
inches.

If these variations are too difficult or cause pain/excess discomfort, use this regression instead. Click "alternate exercise" to view.

Optional Pigeon Length Under Load :

-Work through these progressions slowly. Level 3 and 4 can be very difficult for many athletes.

Level 1 = lying on your back, figure 4 stretch.

Level 2 = pigeon stretch with extended back leg, heavy on hands, slowly lower chest towards knee.

Level 3 = same position, remove hands from floor and keep hands straight out to your side. Lower yourself slowly until chest reaches your knee, slowly raise
back up.

Level 4 = hands to side, lift out and bring back leg around to reach tall kneeling position. Slowly reverse the movement and lower back to starting position.

-Only move onto the next level when you’ve mastered the form of the previous variation and you feel like you need a new challenge
Staff Member Notes:
Hip Flexor Length Under Load:

Level 1 = knee stays on floor, hold onto something for assistance

Level 2 = knee comes off floor (holding in long split squat position), hold onto something for assistance. Find a depth that feels like a stretch but doesn't cause
pain or excessive discomfort

Level 3 = hold in long split squat, begin to oscillate (pulse) up and down just a few inches. We're still holding onto something for assistance.

Level 4 = same position as level 3, small up and down pulse but NO hand assistance.

Level 5 = lightly weighted. Grab a dumbbell or kettlebell and hold it in your hands between your legs (under your hips). Pulse up and down a few inches.

Level 6 = same as level 5, except we use bumper plates or small boxes to stand on in order to get deeper range of motion.

Level 7 = same as level 6, except we add a small turn towards the front leg to stretch the rotational fibers in the back hip flexor.

Regression if these cause pain/discomfort--Click "alternate exercise" to view.




Week 7, Day 5 - Core/ Upper Body
Workout Date: ________________                                                                                         Total Workout Time: ________________

Workout Recommendations
-Today's first workout is focused on upper body and core and the second workout is focused on light full body mobility to drive blood flow and synovial fluid.
-Ideally these two workouts will be separated by at least 6 hours. However, if you don't have the time to separate them, you can perform both workouts at the
same time. Use workout 2 as a warmup for workout 1 if you cannot separate them. The reason we separate them is because it's good for your body to
accumulate movement throughout the day instead of all at once.
-For the isometrics in workout #2, I want you to get through at least 2 sets, but if you have no pain and you're not overly sore from previous lower body days
then you can do up to 4 sets.
Let's get to work!

Staff Member Notes:
90/90 Knee Roll:

Don't worry about counting breaths here. You may simply set your timer and get those hips moving!

*Exercise inspired by Postural Restoration Institute and David Grey

All Four Prone Hip Shift Drill :

1 rep = over and back.

If this drill is too difficult you can use a supine 90/90 knee roll as a regression, just click to view the "alternative exercise".

Cable Rotation/Rib Dissociation:

-If you don’t have access to a cable, use a band for this exercise.

-You should feel a burn in the obliques (side abs), but we shouldn’t be working with too heavy of a weight.

-This exercise should feel rhythmic and fluid.

-Make sure your pelvis doesn’t rotate along with your ribs/spine. Pelvis has to stay locked in place in order to get the dissociation in the ribs/spine.

Bear Crawl (Movement):

4 steps forward (2 with each leg), the 4 steps backwards completes 1 repetition.

If this variation is too difficult or causes pain, you can click the "alternative exercise" to try an All Four Alternating Belly Lift.

*Rotational concepts discussed in the video are inspired by Weck Method.
Staff Member Notes:
Eyes Closed Diagonal Steps :

Level 1 = eyes closed SMALL steps

Level 2 = eyes closed MEDIUM sized steps

Level 3 = eyes closed BIG steps (small bound)

If you are high risk for falls, have poor balance or this exercise causes pain, skip this exercise and try a stationary regression. Click to view "alternate exercise".

Don't worry about counting reps, simply set your timer and get going!

Towel Curl:

If you don't have the towel you can use a rope extension for the cable machine. Or you can use a dumbbell incline curl if you do not have access to a cable
machine. Click "alternate exercise" to view.

Towel Tricep Extension:

If you don't have the towel, you can use a regular rope cable extension as seen in this video. Click "alternate exercise" to view.

Rock Back Breathing:

Reps are counting in breaths

Staff Member Notes:
Toe Motor Control:

Curl the toes, hold for 2 seconds, then lift the toes as high as you can towards your head, hold for 2 seconds, then spread the toes as wide as possible, hold 2
seconds, then repeat the cycle. Perform as many reps as the total set time will allow for.

Wall Side Posture Reach:

*Exercise inspired by ELDOA Technique

Cat Camel:

1 rep = down (cat) and up (camel).

Griffith Wall Rotations:

1 set = completing recommended reps on both sides

*Exercise inspired by Vernon Griffith

Slant Board Isometric Progressions:

Elevate your heel on a weighted plate, small wedge or book if you don’t have a slant board

Level 1 = hand assistance for additional support

Level 2 = bodyweight only

Level 3 = hold a backpack or light dumbbells for additional weight

Always start with a VERY small knee bend. Once you prove you can complete the set pain free you may begin to bend the knee up to 60-80 degrees.

Only progress to the next level if you’re pain free and you are no longer getting an 8/10 burn in the quads by the end of the set

If the slant board iso is too difficult or causes pain, click "alternative exercise" to use the 60 degree iso hold as a regression. The same reps and sets apply to
the regression.

2 Leg Hamstring Iso:

-Lay on your back and bend your knees slightly.

-Drive your heels into the ground to lift your hips off the floor until you achieve full hip extension. Hold in this position for the recommended set time.

-You should feel the burn in your hamstrings, not your lower back. If you feel it in your lower back you are likely hyperextending the spine instead of extending
the hips.

-If you cannot achieve an 8/10 burn in the hamstrings with just your bodyweight, you can move to level 2 by adding weight on top of the hips.

-Find a weight that allows you to achieve an 8/10 burn with no pain in the knees and back
Staff Member Notes:
Resisted Ankle Circles:

-Use a PJF fabric mini band and tie it around a light weight. The weight must be very light to start— 2.5 to 5 lbs is ideal.

-Perform the recommended reps in a clockwise direction, then perform the reps in a counter clockwise direction. Then switch legs to complete the set.

-Only go up in weight if you can no longer achieve an 8/10 burn in the shin/ankle muscles. As always, scale back or skip the exercise if you experience pain in
the ankles.

Hip Flexor/Quad Bridge:

Level 1 = elbows resting on couch, bench or chair.

Level 2 = elbows off, now upper back is resting on couch, bench or chair.

Level 3 = remove couch, bench or chair. Extend arms and use hands to support you on the floor.

Level 4 = use elbows/forearms to support you on the floor.

Some people experience knee pain with these variations. If level 1 knee pain or any other joint pain, use this regression instead-- Click "alternate exercise" to
view.

Workout Recommendations
-As always, skip any exercise (or choose the regression) if it causes pain or discomfort.

-Remember, you have to train according to your current level. This is a general blueprint, but YOU have to know when to skip certain things that may not fit
your current level. Training hard is important, but training SMART is key!

Staff Member Notes:
Nasal Breathing Cardio Warm Up:

If you're pain free, you can use a fast walk/jog as your warmup. Start slow and gradually ramp it up to a jogging pace. However, if you haven't been running
consistently or you have pain when running, then you can use a fast walk OR a stationary bike, elliptical or any other low impact cardio machine.

Split Stance Loaded Hip Rotations :

-Use a light dumbbell or any form of additional weight 5-15 lbs is plenty of weight.

-Hold onto a med ball or bench with the off hand for some added stability.

-Complete the reps with right leg forward and right hand, THEN right leg forward left hand, THEN left leg forward left hand, THEN left leg forward right hand.
That completes 1 set.

-For example, if it says 8 reps that means RIGHT LEG forward with band around right thigh-- do 8 reps right hand THEN 8 reps left hand. Then switch the band
to the left leg, put your left leg forward and complete 8 reps left hand then 8 reps right hand. A total of 32 repetitions per set.

Hip Shift Squat:

If this position is too difficult for you to master, you can use this regression-- Click "alternate exercise" to view".
Staff Member Notes:
Half Kneeling Hip Flexor Stretch:

1 set = completing recommended reps to both sides.

If you don't have enough flexibility to grab your foot, you can use the alternate exercise as a regression for 5 reps (breaths). Click "alternate exercise" to view.

Ballistic Wall Side Hip Extension:

If you do not feel ready for the dynamic (fast) variation of this exercise. You can start slow and controlled.

Hip Loaded Diagonal Jumps :

Level 1 = bodyweight. Start with small side to side jumps and gradually increase distance.

Level 2 = lightly loaded. 5-10 lb dumbbells.

1 repetition = over and back (left and right side)

Mutli-direction Bounds:

If this is too difficult for you, regress back to this Euro Step Balance drill. Click "alternate exercise" to view.

RDL Row w/ Rotation :

If this variation is too difficult or you don't have access to a cable machine you can use the 1 leg dumbbell RDL as a regression. Click "alternate exercise" to
view.

Knee Step Overs:

Level 1 = bodyweight with small range of motion and small step length. Gradually work to get more range of motion and longer step length.

Level 2 = dumbbells in each hand 5-25 lbs to start.

Level 3 = keep the same dumbbell weight and add a resistance band pulling you forward (or use a cable machine/belt around hips).

1 set = completion of recommended reps on each side
Staff Member Notes:
Lateral Crossover Sled Drag Fatigue/Coordination :

Perform the sled crossover drag (or band crossover as a substitution) for the recommended reps (measured in time) on BOTH sides. Then without taking a
break, immediately begin the carioca to challenge coordination under fatigue.

Remember, the carioca coordination drill is performed for HALF the reps (time) of the weighted drag. So if the recommended reps said 40 seconds, that means
40 seconds down with the weighted drag, switch sides, 40 seconds back. Then 20 seconds on the unloaded carioca. Then take your rest period before
beginning your next set.

If you don't have the sled, perform with a band (have a friend walk with you resisting you with the band). Or you can do a stationary cable or band crossover
step, come back to starting position then repeat.

Hip Flexor Length Under Load:

Level 1 = knee stays on floor, hold onto something for assistance

Level 2 = knee comes off floor (holding in long split squat position), hold onto something for assistance. Find a depth that feels like a stretch but doesn't cause
pain or excessive discomfort

Level 3 = hold in long split squat, begin to oscillate (pulse) up and down just a few inches. We're still holding onto something for assistance.

Level 4 = same position as level 3, small up and down pulse but NO hand assistance.

Level 5 = lightly weighted. Grab a dumbbell or kettlebell and hold it in your hands between your legs (under your hips). Pulse up and down a few inches.

Level 6 = same as level 5, except we use bumper plates or small boxes to stand on in order to get deeper range of motion.

Level 7 = same as level 6, except we add a small turn towards the front leg to stretch the rotational fibers in the back hip flexor.

Regression if these cause pain/discomfort--Click "alternate exercise" to view.

Wide Stance Length Under Load :

If this exercise causes discomfort or pain, or simply makes you feel even more tight after you finish you may not need additional hamstring stretching and you
can skip this exercise.

Rotational RDL Length Under Load :

If this exercise causes discomfort or pain, you can skip it or try an unloaded post chain stretch as a regression. Click "alternate exercise" to view.
Staff Member Notes:
Lateral Lunge Length Under Load :

Level 1 = feet on floor

Level 2 = non loaded leg elevated on bumper plate or small step 3-6 inches

Level 3 = non loaded leg elevated on box, bench or chair around mid shin to knee height

Level 4 = non loaded leg elevated on box, bench or chair around mid shin to knee height AND the loaded leg elevated on bumper plate or small step 3-6
inches.

If these variations are too difficult or cause pain/excess discomfort, use this regression instead. Click "alternate exercise" to view.

Optional Pigeon Length Under Load :

-Work through these progressions slowly. Level 3 and 4 can be very difficult for many athletes.

Level 1 = lying on your back, figure 4 stretch.

Level 2 = pigeon stretch with extended back leg, heavy on hands, slowly lower chest towards knee.

Level 3 = same position, remove hands from floor and keep hands straight out to your side. Lower yourself slowly until chest reaches your knee, slowly raise
back up.

Level 4 = hands to side, lift out and bring back leg around to reach tall kneeling position. Slowly reverse the movement and lower back to starting position.

-Only move onto the next level when you’ve mastered the form of the previous variation and you feel like you need a new challenge

Click to view the "alternative exercise" for be another beginner level alternative

One Leg RDL Rotation w/ Toes Off :

If this is too difficult unassisted, you can try this regression instead. Click "alternate exercise" to view.

Crossover Step Adductor Stretch:

1 rep = over and back

If this is too advanced or causes pain, you can try the static adductor stretch progressions instead. Click "alternate exercise" to view.

Long Length Hamstring Rapid Loading :

-Level 1 = Skip with toe reaching mid shin level

-Level 2 = Straight leg bound with toe reaching shin level

-Level 3 = Straight leg bound pushing for bigger distance

Curved Tempo Runs :

1 set = down 30 yards AND back 30 yards, THEN take your rest period before your next set.

Use 3 cones spaced 10 yards apart.

If you are 100% injury free and have been sprinting consistently, you can skip to 80-90% max speed in week 1. Then in week 2 ramp it up to 90-100%.

But if you have not been sprinting consistently and/or you're coming back from an injury you may want to start at 50% speed week 1, 60-70% week 2, 70-90%
week 3 and 90-100% week 4. Only progress intensities if you're pain free.

Short Accel/Decel Progressions:

Level 1= 5 yard acceleration + 5 yards of room to decelerate.

Level 2= 5 yard acceleration + only 2 yards of room to decelerate.

1 set = completion of recommended reps to one side , then completion of recommended reps to the other side.
Staff Member Notes:
Hip Flexor Length Under Load:

Level 1 = knee stays on floor, hold onto something for assistance

Level 2 = knee comes off floor (holding in long split squat position), hold onto something for assistance. Find a depth that feels like a stretch but doesn't cause
pain or excessive discomfort

Level 3 = hold in long split squat, begin to oscillate (pulse) up and down just a few inches. We're still holding onto something for assistance.

Level 4 = same position as level 3, small up and down pulse but NO hand assistance.

Level 5 = lightly weighted. Grab a dumbbell or kettlebell and hold it in your hands between your legs (under your hips). Pulse up and down a few inches.

Level 6 = same as level 5, except we use bumper plates or small boxes to stand on in order to get deeper range of motion.

Level 7 = same as level 6, except we add a small turn towards the front leg to stretch the rotational fibers in the back hip flexor.

Regression if these cause pain/discomfort--Click "alternate exercise" to view.

Lateral Lunge Length Under Load :

Level 1 = feet on floor

Level 2 = non loaded leg elevated on bumper plate or small step 3-6 inches

Level 3 = non loaded leg elevated on box, bench or chair around mid shin to knee height

Level 4 = non loaded leg elevated on box, bench or chair around mid shin to knee height AND the loaded leg elevated on bumper plate or small step 3-6
inches.

If these variations are too difficult or cause pain/excess discomfort, use this regression instead. Click "alternate exercise" to view.

Optional Pigeon Length Under Load :

-Work through these progressions slowly. Level 3 and 4 can be very difficult for many athletes.

Level 1 = lying on your back, figure 4 stretch.

Level 2 = pigeon stretch with extended back leg, heavy on hands, slowly lower chest towards knee.

Level 3 = same position, remove hands from floor and keep hands straight out to your side. Lower yourself slowly until chest reaches your knee, slowly raise
back up.

Level 4 = hands to side, lift out and bring back leg around to reach tall kneeling position. Slowly reverse the movement and lower back to starting position.

-Only move onto the next level when you’ve mastered the form of the previous variation and you feel like you need a new challenge



Week 8, Day 2 - Core/ Upper Body
Workout Date: ________________                                                                                     Total Workout Time: ________________

Workout Recommendations
-Today's first workout is focused on upper body and core and the second workout is focused on light full body mobility to drive blood flow and synovial fluid.
-Ideally these two workouts will be separated by at least 6 hours. However, if you don't have the time to separate them, you can perform both workouts at the
same time. Use workout 2 as a warmup for workout 1 if you cannot separate them. The reason we separate them is because it's good for your body to
accumulate movement throughout the day instead of all at once.
-For the isometrics in workout #2, I want you to get through at least 2 sets, but if you have no pain and you're not overly sore from previous lower body days
then you can do up to 4 sets.
Let's get to work!

Staff Member Notes:
90/90 Knee Roll:

Don't worry about counting breaths here. You may simply set your timer and get those hips moving!

*Exercise inspired by Postural Restoration Institute and David Grey
Staff Member Notes:
All Four Prone Hip Shift Drill :

1 rep = over and back.

If this drill is too difficult you can use a supine 90/90 knee roll as a regression, just click to view the "alternative exercise".

Cable Rotation/Rib Dissociation:

-If you don’t have access to a cable, use a band for this exercise.

-You should feel a burn in the obliques (side abs), but we shouldn’t be working with too heavy of a weight.

-This exercise should feel rhythmic and fluid.

-Make sure your pelvis doesn’t rotate along with your ribs/spine. Pelvis has to stay locked in place in order to get the dissociation in the ribs/spine.

Bear Crawl (Movement):

4 steps forward (2 with each leg), the 4 steps backwards completes 1 repetition.

If this variation is too difficult or causes pain, you can click the "alternative exercise" to try an All Four Alternating Belly Lift.

*Rotational concepts discussed in the video are inspired by Weck Method.

Eyes Closed Diagonal Steps :

Level 1 = eyes closed SMALL steps

Level 2 = eyes closed MEDIUM sized steps

Level 3 = eyes closed BIG steps (small bound)

If you are high risk for falls, have poor balance or this exercise causes pain, skip this exercise and try a stationary regression. Click to view "alternate exercise".

Don't worry about counting reps, simply set your timer and get going!

Towel Curl:

If you don't have the towel you can use a rope extension for the cable machine. Or you can use a dumbbell incline curl if you do not have access to a cable

If you don't have the towel, you can use a regular rope cable extension as seen in this video. Click "alternate exercise" to view.

Staff Member Notes:
Toe Motor Control:

Curl the toes, hold for 2 seconds, then lift the toes as high as you can towards your head, hold for 2 seconds, then spread the toes as wide as possible, hold 2
seconds, then repeat the cycle. Perform as many reps as the total set time will allow for.

Wall Side Posture Reach:

*Exercise inspired by ELDOA Technique


Cat Camel:

1 rep = down (cat) and up (camel).

Griffith Wall Rotations:

1 set = completing recommended reps on both sides

*Exercise inspired by Vernon Griffith
Staff Member Notes:
Slant Board Isometric Progressions:

Elevate your heel on a weighted plate, small wedge or book if you don’t have a slant board

Level 1 = hand assistance for additional support

Level 2 = bodyweight only

Level 3 = hold a backpack or light dumbbells for additional weight

Always start with a VERY small knee bend. Once you prove you can complete the set pain free you may begin to bend the knee up to 60-80 degrees.

Only progress to the next level if you’re pain free and you are no longer getting an 8/10 burn in the quads by the end of the set

If the slant board iso is too difficult or causes pain, click "alternative exercise" to use the 60 degree iso hold as a regression. The same reps and sets apply to
the regression.

2 Leg Hamstring Iso:

-Lay on your back and bend your knees slightly.

-Drive your heels into the ground to lift your hips off the floor until you achieve full hip extension. Hold in this position for the recommended set time.

-You should feel the burn in your hamstrings, not your lower back. If you feel it in your lower back you are likely hyperextending the spine instead of extending
the hips.

-If you cannot achieve an 8/10 burn in the hamstrings with just your bodyweight, you can move to level 2 by adding weight on top of the hips.

-Find a weight that allows you to achieve an 8/10 burn with no pain in the knees and back

Resisted Ankle Circles:

-Use a PJF fabric mini band and tie it around a light weight. The weight must be very light to start— 2.5 to 5 lbs is ideal.

-Perform the recommended reps in a clockwise direction, then perform the reps in a counter clockwise direction. Then switch legs to complete the set.

-Only go up in weight if you can no longer achieve an 8/10 burn in the shin/ankle muscles. As always, scale back or skip the exercise if you experience pain in
the ankles.
Staff Member Notes:
Hip Flexor/Quad Bridge:

Level 1 = elbows resting on couch, bench or chair.

Level 2 = elbows off, now upper back is resting on couch, bench or chair.

Level 3 = remove couch, bench or chair. Extend arms and use hands to support you on the floor.

Level 4 = use elbows/forearms to support you on the floor.

Some people experience knee pain with these variations. If level 1 knee pain or any other joint pain, use this regression instead-- Click "alternate exercise" to
view.

Optional Foam Roll Circuit:

25-45 seconds each

Supine Cross Connect:

reps = breaths (inhale through the nose, long exhale out the mouth).
Staff Member Notes:
Rolling Arm Bar w/ Screwdriver Rotation :

This drill works miracles for building hip internal rotation mobility, but the details can be a bit tricky. It’s ok if you don’t get it down day 1.

Follow the directions in the video but I want you to especially think about the following tips when you perform the exercise.

The leg that is down on the ground must be actively pushed into the floor throughout the entire exercise. Don’t lose pressure in that leg or you won’t get the
hip mobility benefits.

Breath in through the nose as you roll over, and out through the mouth when you roll back to the starting position.

Keep the knee that is down fully locked out and the foot dorsiflexed (toes pulled up towards your head).

Make sure the additional weight is light, between 10 and 20 lbs.

Walking Sprinters Mobility:

1 set = completion of recommended reps on each leg.

*Exercise idea inspired by compound performance

Extensive Locomotion Cariocas:

As always, if this exercise cause pain, skip it and move on to the next exercise.

Level 1 = foot cycles over ANKLE level (low)

Level 2 = foot cycles over MID SHIN level (medium)

Level 3 = foot cycles over KNEE level (High)

Level 4 = weighted vest or medicine ball + KNEE level cycle (High)

Backwards Extensive Locomotive Skips :

Level 1 = backwards skips foot coming over ANKLE
Level 2 = backwards skips foot coming over MID SHIN
Level 3 = backwards skips foot coming over KNEE
Level 4 = WEIGHTED vest or dumbbells + backwards skips foot coming over KNEE
Staff Member Notes:
Extensive Locomotive Skips:

As always, if this exercise cause pain, skip it and move on to the next exercise.

Level 1 = foot cycles over ANKLE level (low)

Level 2 = foot cycles over MID SHIN level (medium)

Level 3 = foot cycles at KNEE level (High)

Level 4 = weighted vest or medicine ball + KNEE level cycle (High)

Wide Stance Length Under Load :

If this exercise causes discomfort or pain, or simply makes you feel even more tight after you finish you may not need additional hamstring stretching and you
can skip this exercise.



Week 8, Day 4 - Lower Body
Workout Date: ________________                                                                                      Total Workout Time: ________________

Workout Recommendations

Time to get some BIG GAINS! Let's get to work!! A few notes for today’s workout before you get started:
-I don't want you to get overly sore from this workout. We want to feel a LITTLE soreness tomorrow, but if we're overly sore then we did too much too soon.
-As always, skip any exercise (or choose the regression) if it causes pain or discomfort.
-If you're advanced and have been highly active for the last month, it's ok to add sets wherever you feel you need for some extra work.
-Remember, you have to train according to your current level. This is a general blueprint, but YOU have to know when to skip certain things that may not fit
your current level. Training hard is important, but training SMART is key!

The journey begins now! Let's GO!

Staff Member Notes:
Nasal Breathing Cardio Warm Up:

If you're pain free, you can use a fast walk/jog as your warmup. Start slow and gradually ramp it up to a jogging pace. However, if you haven't been running
consistently or you have pain when running, then you can use a fast walk OR a stationary bike, elliptical or any other low impact cardio machine.

Split Stance Loaded Hip Rotations :

-Use a light dumbbell or any form of additional weight 5-15 lbs is plenty of weight.

-Hold onto a med ball or bench with the off hand for some added stability.

-Complete the reps with right leg forward and right hand, THEN right leg forward left hand, THEN left leg forward left hand, THEN left leg forward right hand.
That completes 1 set.

-For example, if it says 8 reps that means RIGHT LEG forward with band around right thigh-- do 8 reps right hand THEN 8 reps left hand. Then switch the band
to the left leg, put your left leg forward and complete 8 reps left hand then 8 reps right hand. A total of 32 repetitions per set.

Hip Shift Squat:

If this position is too difficult for you to master, you can use this regression-- Click "alternate exercise" to view".
Staff Member Notes:
Half Kneeling Hip Flexor Stretch:

1 set = completing recommended reps to both sides.

If you don't have enough flexibility to grab your foot, you can use the alternate exercise as a regression for 5 reps (breaths). Click "alternate exercise" to view.

Ballistic Wall Side Hip Extension:

If you do not feel ready for the dynamic (fast) variation of this exercise. You can start slow and controlled.

Click to view the "alternative exercise" for be another beginner level alternative

Walking Sprinters Mobility:

1 set = completion of recommended reps on each leg.

*Exercise idea inspired by compound performance

Crossover Step Adductor Stretch:

Curved Tempo Runs :

If you are 100% injury free and have been sprinting consistently, you can skip to 80-90% max speed in week 1. Then in week 2 ramp it up to 90-100%.

But if you have not been sprinting consistently and/or you're coming back from an injury you may want to start at 50% speed week 1, 60-70% week 2, 70-90%
week 3 and 90-100% week 4. Only progress intensities if you're pain free.


Altitude Drop Progressions:

-If you are able to jump maximally pain free, you can skip straight to expert level on this drill. However, if jumping and landing still causes pain in your joints
you may want to start at beginner/intermediate level and prove to yourself you can do this drill pain free.

-Beginner Level = Start at 50% of your standing vertical jump height, progress by 2 inches per week.

-Intermediate Level = Start at 50% of your standing vertical jump height, progress by 2 inches per week.

-Expert = Start with a box height of your standing vertical jump height. Progress box height by 4 inches per week.
Staff Member Notes:
RFESS Progessions:

If this exercise causes pain or discomfort, you can regress back to isometrics. Click "alternate exercise" to view.

Eccentric Nordic Hamstring Curl :

-Level 1 = perform the eccentric (lowering) but use your hands to push back up. Don’t perform the upward phase.

-Level 2 = perform the eccentric and the concentric but use a push off with the hands to help yourself get up.

-Level 3 = add weight on the way down, set the weight down and push off with your hands to get back up.

Wide Stance Length Under Load :

If this exercise causes discomfort or pain, or simply makes you feel even more tight after you finish you may not need additional hamstring stretching and you
can skip this exercise.

Lateral Lunge Length Under Load :

Level 1 = feet on floor

Level 2 = non loaded leg elevated on bumper plate or small step 3-6 inches

Level 3 = non loaded leg elevated on box, bench or chair around mid shin to knee height

Level 4 = non loaded leg elevated on box, bench or chair around mid shin to knee height AND the loaded leg elevated on bumper plate or small step 3-6
inches.

If these variations are too difficult or cause pain/excess discomfort, use this regression instead. Click "alternate exercise" to view.

-Only move onto the next level when you’ve mastered the form of the previous variation and you feel like you need a new challenge
Staff Member Notes:
Hip Flexor Length Under Load:

Level 1 = knee stays on floor, hold onto something for assistance

Level 2 = knee comes off floor (holding in long split squat position), hold onto something for assistance. Find a depth that feels like a stretch but doesn't cause
pain or excessive discomfort

Level 3 = hold in long split squat, begin to oscillate (pulse) up and down just a few inches. We're still holding onto something for assistance.

Level 4 = same position as level 3, small up and down pulse but NO hand assistance.

Staff Member Notes:
Toe Motor Control:

Curl the toes, hold for 2 seconds, then lift the toes as high as you can towards your head, hold for 2 seconds, then spread the toes as wide as possible, hold 2
seconds, then repeat the cycle. Perform as many reps as the total set time will allow for.

Wall Side Posture Reach:

*Exercise inspired by ELDOA Technique

Cat Camel:

1 rep = down (cat) and up (camel).

Griffith Wall Rotations:

1 set = completing recommended reps on both sides

*Exercise inspired by Vernon Griffith

Slant Board Isometric Progressions:

Elevate your heel on a weighted plate, small wedge or book if you don’t have a slant board

Level 1 = hand assistance for additional support

Level 2 = bodyweight only

Level 3 = hold a backpack or light dumbbells for additional weight

Always start with a VERY small knee bend. Once you prove you can complete the set pain free you may begin to bend the knee up to 60-80 degrees.

Only progress to the next level if you’re pain free and you are no longer getting an 8/10 burn in the quads by the end of the set

If the slant board iso is too difficult or causes pain, click "alternative exercise" to use the 60 degree iso hold as a regression. The same reps and sets apply to
the regression.
Staff Member Notes:
2 Leg Hamstring Iso:

-Lay on your back and bend your knees slightly.

-Drive your heels into the ground to lift your hips off the floor until you achieve full hip extension. Hold in this position for the recommended set time.

-You should feel the burn in your hamstrings, not your lower back. If you feel it in your lower back you are likely hyperextending the spine instead of extending
the hips.

-If you cannot achieve an 8/10 burn in the hamstrings with just your bodyweight, you can move to level 2 by adding weight on top of the hips.

-Find a weight that allows you to achieve an 8/10 burn with no pain in the knees and back

Resisted Ankle Circles:

-Use a PJF fabric mini band and tie it around a light weight. The weight must be very light to start— 2.5 to 5 lbs is ideal.

-Perform the recommended reps in a clockwise direction, then perform the reps in a counter clockwise direction. Then switch legs to complete the set.

-Only go up in weight if you can no longer achieve an 8/10 burn in the shin/ankle muscles. As always, scale back or skip the exercise if you experience pain in
the ankles.

Hip Flexor/Quad Bridge:

Level 1 = elbows resting on couch, bench or chair.

Level 2 = elbows off, now upper back is resting on couch, bench or chair.

Level 3 = remove couch, bench or chair. Extend arms and use hands to support you on the floor.

Level 4 = use elbows/forearms to support you on the floor.

Some people experience knee pain with these variations. If level 1 knee pain or any other joint pain, use this regression instead-- Click "alternate exercise" to
view.

Half Kneeling Hip Flexor Stretch:

1 set = completing recommended reps to both sides.

If you don't have enough flexibility to grab your foot, you can use the alternate exercise as a regression for 5 reps (breaths). Click "alternate exercise" to view.



Week 8, Day 6 - Lower Body
Workout Date: ________________                                                                                          Total Workout Time: ________________

Workout Recommendations

-As always, skip any exercise (or choose the regression) if it causes pain or discomfort.
-If you're advanced and have been highly active for the last month, it's ok to add sets wherever you feel you need for some extra work.
-Remember, you have to train according to your current level. This is a general blueprint, but YOU have to know when to skip certain things that may not fit
your current level. Training hard is important, but training SMART is key!

Staff Member Notes:
Nasal Breathing Cardio Warm Up:

If you're pain free, you can use a fast walk/jog as your warmup. Start slow and gradually ramp it up to a jogging pace. However, if you haven't been running
consistently or you have pain when running, then you can use a fast walk OR a stationary bike, elliptical or any other low impact cardio machine.
Staff Member Notes:
Split Stance Loaded Hip Rotations :

-Use a light dumbbell or any form of additional weight 5-15 lbs is plenty of weight.

-Hold onto a med ball or bench with the off hand for some added stability.

-Complete the reps with right leg forward and right hand, THEN right leg forward left hand, THEN left leg forward left hand, THEN left leg forward right hand.
That completes 1 set.

-For example, if it says 8 reps that means RIGHT LEG forward with band around right thigh-- do 8 reps right hand THEN 8 reps left hand. Then switch the band
to the left leg, put your left leg forward and complete 8 reps left hand then 8 reps right hand. A total of 32 repetitions per set.

Hip Shift Squat:

If this position is too difficult for you to master, you can use this regression-- Click "alternate exercise" to view".

Half Kneeling Hip Flexor Stretch:

1 set = completing recommended reps to both sides.

If you don't have enough flexibility to grab your foot, you can use the alternate exercise as a regression for 5 reps (breaths). Click "alternate exercise" to view.

Ballistic Wall Side Hip Extension:

If you do not feel ready for the dynamic (fast) variation of this exercise. You can start slow and controlled.

Click to view the "alternative exercise" for be another beginner level alternative

Hip Loaded Diagonal Jumps :

Level 1 = bodyweight. Start with small side to side jumps and gradually increase distance.

Level 2 = lightly loaded. 5-10 lb dumbbells.

1 repetition = over and back (left and right side)

Mutli-direction Bounds:

If this is too difficult for you, regress back to this Euro Step Balance drill. Click "alternate exercise" to view.

RDL Row w/ Rotation :

If this variation is too difficult or you don't have access to a cable machine you can use the 1 leg dumbbell RDL as a regression. Click "alternate exercise" to
view.
Staff Member Notes:
Knee Step Overs:

Level 1 = bodyweight with small range of motion and small step length. Gradually work to get more range of motion and longer step length.

Level 2 = dumbbells in each hand 5-25 lbs to start.

Level 3 = keep the same dumbbell weight and add a resistance band pulling you forward (or use a cable machine/belt around hips).

1 set = completion of recommended reps on each side

Lateral Crossover Sled Drag Fatigue/Coordination :

Perform the sled crossover drag (or band crossover as a substitution) for the recommended reps (measured in time) on BOTH sides. Then without taking a
break, immediately begin the carioca to challenge coordination under fatigue.

Remember, the carioca coordination drill is performed for HALF the reps (time) of the weighted drag. So if the recommended reps said 40 seconds, that means
40 seconds down with the weighted drag, switch sides, 40 seconds back. Then 20 seconds on the unloaded carioca. Then take your rest period before
beginning your next set.

If you don't have the sled, perform with a band (have a friend walk with you resisting you with the band). Or you can do a stationary cable or band crossover
step, come back to starting position then repeat.


Hip Flexor Length Under Load:

Level 1 = knee stays on floor, hold onto something for assistance

Level 2 = knee comes off floor (holding in long split squat position), hold onto something for assistance. Find a depth that feels like a stretch but doesn't cause
pain or excessive discomfort

Level 3 = hold in long split squat, begin to oscillate (pulse) up and down just a few inches. We're still holding onto something for assistance.

Level 4 = same position as level 3, small up and down pulse but NO hand assistance.

Level 5 = lightly weighted. Grab a dumbbell or kettlebell and hold it in your hands between your legs (under your hips). Pulse up and down a few inches.

Level 6 = same as level 5, except we use bumper plates or small boxes to stand on in order to get deeper range of motion.

Level 7 = same as level 6, except we add a small turn towards the front leg to stretch the rotational fibers in the back hip flexor.

Regression if these cause pain/discomfort--Click "alternate exercise" to view.
Wide Stance Length Under Load :

If this exercise causes discomfort or pain, or simply makes you feel even more tight after you finish you may not need additional hamstring stretching and you
can skip this exercise.
Staff Member Notes:
Rotational RDL Length Under Load :

If this exercise causes discomfort or pain, you can skip it or try an unloaded post chain stretch as a regression. Click "alternate exercise" to view.

Lateral Lunge Length Under Load :

Level 1 = feet on floor

Level 2 = non loaded leg elevated on bumper plate or small step 3-6 inches

Level 3 = non loaded leg elevated on box, bench or chair around mid shin to knee height

Level 4 = non loaded leg elevated on box, bench or chair around mid shin to knee height AND the loaded leg elevated on bumper plate or small step 3-6
inches.

If these variations are too difficult or cause pain/excess discomfort, use this regression instead. Click "alternate exercise" to view.

Optional Pigeon Length Under Load :

-Work through these progressions slowly. Level 3 and 4 can be very difficult for many athletes.

Level 1 = lying on your back, figure 4 stretch.

Level 2 = pigeon stretch with extended back leg, heavy on hands, slowly lower chest towards knee.

Level 3 = same position, remove hands from floor and keep hands straight out to your side. Lower yourself slowly until chest reaches your knee, slowly raise
back up.

Level 4 = hands to side, lift out and bring back leg around to reach tall kneeling position. Slowly reverse the movement and lower back to starting position.

-Only move onto the next level when you’ve mastered the form of the previous variation and you feel like you need a new challenge
